An obstacle to the abuse of the Institute of Legislative Emergency is merely the legal definition of the reasons on the basis of which this situation can be declared. According to the Constitutional Court, these reasons must be consistent with the Constitution and must be reviewed. By the way, the Constitutional Court also did not recognise the potential threat of obstruction of opposition Members as an exceptional condition within the meaning of the House's Rules of Procedure, which justifies the use of the state of legislative emergency. In other words, if the ruling coalition would use the institute of legislative emergency because it fears that the opposition will obstruct, which has already happened in the past, it has not succeeded in the Constitutional Court. It didn't succeed. The opposition has its right, its rights, and the Constitutional Court has acknowledged it. [[2.00 hours]] If the opposition objects to non-compliance with the standard legislative procedure for the adoption of new laws, no element of extraordinaryness can be seen in these objections. That means what we're doing now. We're objecting here, explaining why, in our opinion, the legislative emergency is wrong in applying this law, I've been doing this for a long time, I'm explaining it here in the adoption of this law. And the Constitutional Court makes that clear. Once again: If the opposition raises objections to non-compliance with the standard legislative procedure in adopting new laws, this means a normal legislative process with three readings, no element of extraordinaryness can be seen in these objections. The opposition, according to the Constitutional Court, in this case, merely exercised its right to protect its rights in accordance with the Rules of Procedure. Because the opposition, if you want to accept this in a legislative emergency, in our opinion, there is no reason for it, and if you shorten the process, the opposition, according to the Constitutional Court, is losing its rights to intervene and discuss in individual readings of the legislative process. In the light of the above arguments, it is clear that the negotiation of an amendment to the Act on Pension Insurance under the regime of legislative emergency is contrary to constitutional principles, constitutional practice and the case law of the Constitutional Court, which I consider to be extremely serious. So the best solution to this unsustainable situation is to discuss this government proposal properly in three readings, or to reject it directly. As the Constitutional Court has repeatedly stated, it is not enough to examine only whether there has been a breach of the rules of procedure of the two chambers of Parliament or of other procedural rules in order to assess whether the legislation was adopted by law. The function of the Constitutional Court is mainly the protection of constitutionality and therefore it has to assess whether the Constitution of the Czech Republic has also complied with the requirements for the adoption of the law. In this case, this is the question of the retroactivity of the present law, the principle of legal certainty and legitimate expectations of the addressees of legal standards, but also the protection of the social rights of pensioners, which are the fundamental human rights that I was talking about here at the beginning of my speech, and the protection of the rights to exercise the parliamentary mandate of each Member, whether in a coalition or in opposition. The exercise of the legislative procedure must take into account a number of constitutional principles, in particular the principles of the division of power, pluralism, free competition of political forces, the principle of representative democracy based on the free exercise of the mandate and equality of Members of Parliament, freedom of expression and free parliamentary debate, the principle of majority decision-making and the inseparable principle of protection of minorities, which is represented by us on parliamentary ground, the parliamentary opposition. These fundamental principles also include the originally natural rule of law to hear all parties, and finally the requirement of public and transparent deliberation of the law. The Institute limits or relativizes all these constitutional principles and therefore the conditions for its application must be interpreted very strictly. The state of legislative emergency can therefore be used only for situations that are clearly and significantly out of line at first sight with the normal course of political processes and political disputes between the government and the opposition. The existence of such an exceptional serious situation must have a reasonable basis and must be supported by clear facts. Ladies and gentlemen, our movement does not happen to have freedom and democracy in its name. These are fundamental values for us, as well as respect for the Constitution. I have repeatedly quoted here from the findings of the Constitutional Court, which are a binding source of law, and I am actually convinced that the most sensible thing we should do now is to abolish the state of legislative emergency.
